1. [ Direct Connect ] A company requires a dedicated private connection from its on-premises data center to AWS with consistent sub-10ms latency and 5 Gbps bandwidth. What is the most appropriate solution? ; Order an AWS Direct Connect Hosted Connection (5 Gbps) from a Direct Connect Partner. <br><br> *AWS Direct Connect provides dedicated, private fiber connectivity that bypasses the public internet, delivering consistent latency and guaranteed bandwidth.<br> *Site-to-Site VPN uses the public internet which introduces variable latency and is limited to ~1.25 Gbps, making it unsuitable for this requirement.<br> *Hosted connections support 50 Mbps to 10 Gbps, so 5 Gbps is available through a partner; Dedicated connections only come in 1, 10, or 100 Gbps.<br>

2. [ Direct Connect ] A company has a single Direct Connect connection and wants to maximize resiliency to prevent any single point of failure. What should they do? ; Implement the Maximum Resiliency model: two DX connections at two separate DX locations, each terminating on different AWS devices. <br><br> *The Maximum Resiliency model requires connections at two physically separate DX locations with separate devices at each location, eliminating all single points of failure.<br> *A single connection or two connections at the same location still has location-level SPOF and does not meet maximum resiliency requirements.<br> *AWS recommends this for mission-critical applications like financial services where any interruption is unacceptable.<br>

3. [ Direct Connect ] An enterprise needs to connect its single Direct Connect connection to multiple VPCs across three AWS Regions. What is the most cost-effective solution? ; Create a Direct Connect Gateway and associate it with Virtual Private Gateways (VGWs) in each VPC across the three regions. <br><br> *Direct Connect Gateway is a globally-scoped resource that enables a single DX connection to reach VPCs in multiple AWS Regions without requiring separate DX connections per region.<br> *Without DX Gateway, you would need separate DX connections per region, greatly increasing cost.<br> *DX Gateway does NOT allow VPC-to-VPC routing; it only enables on-premises to VPC connectivity, so if inter-VPC routing is needed, Transit Gateway must also be used.<br>

4. [ Direct Connect ] A company wants to encrypt all data traversing their Direct Connect connection for compliance. What is the recommended approach? ; Run an IPSec VPN tunnel over the Direct Connect connection using a Public Virtual Interface. <br><br> *Direct Connect does not encrypt traffic by default; MACsec is only available on Dedicated 10G/100G connections and encrypts at Layer 2.<br> *Running IPSec VPN over a Public VIF provides both the consistent bandwidth of DX and encryption required for compliance.<br> *This approach provides private connectivity with encryption: DX gives you the dedicated path, VPN adds the encryption layer on top.<br>

5. [ Direct Connect ] What is the maximum number of routes AWS will advertise to a customer's router over a single BGP session on a Direct Connect Private VIF? ; 1,000 routes. <br><br> *AWS has a hard limit of 1,000 BGP routes per BGP session on Direct Connect Private and Transit VIFs.<br> *Exceeding this limit causes AWS to terminate the BGP session, which would drop all connectivity over that VIF.<br> *To stay within limits, use route summarization/aggregation on the AWS side and filter unnecessary routes.<br>

6. [ Direct Connect ] A company has both a Direct Connect connection and a Site-to-Site VPN to the same VPC. They want DX to be the preferred path for all traffic, with VPN as backup. How should BGP be configured? ; Set a lower MED (Multi-Exit Discriminator) value on the DX BGP session to make it more preferred. Use AS Path Prepending on the VPN BGP session to make it less preferred from the AWS side. <br><br> *BGP uses MED to influence inbound path selection at the remote AS; lower MED = more preferred, so setting MED=100 on DX and MED=200 on VPN makes DX preferred.<br> *AS Path Prepending appends the customer ASN multiple times on the VPN advertisement, making the AS path longer and therefore less preferred by AWS's BGP route selection.<br> *When DX fails, BGP withdraws DX routes automatically, and traffic fails over to the VPN path within BGP convergence time (typically seconds to minutes).<br>

7. [ Direct Connect ] What are the three types of Virtual Interfaces (VIFs) available on Direct Connect and when is each used? ; Private VIF (connect to VPC private resources via VGW), Public VIF (access AWS public service endpoints), Transit VIF (connect to Transit Gateway). <br><br> *Private VIF is used for accessing resources within a VPC using private IP addresses; it connects to a Virtual Private Gateway or DX Gateway.<br> *Public VIF provides access to all AWS public IP space including S3, EC2 public IPs, and other public endpoints without traversing the internet.<br> *Transit VIF is used specifically with Direct Connect Gateway when connecting to a Transit Gateway, enabling access to multiple VPCs and on-premises networks in a hub-and-spoke model.<br>

8. [ Direct Connect ] A company wants to aggregate four 10 Gbps Direct Connect connections into a single 40 Gbps logical connection. What AWS feature enables this? ; Link Aggregation Group (LAG) using LACP (Link Aggregation Control Protocol). <br><br> *LAG allows bundling multiple physical DX connections into one logical connection using LACP for increased bandwidth and active/active load sharing.<br> *All connections in a LAG must be the same bandwidth, at the same DX location, and terminate on the same AWS device — mixing 1G and 10G is not allowed.<br> *Maximum of 4 dedicated connections per LAG; the logical bandwidth is the sum of all member connections.<br>

9. [ Direct Connect ] What is the difference between a Dedicated Connection and a Hosted Connection in Direct Connect? ; Dedicated Connection is ordered directly from AWS (1/10/100 Gbps), you own the port. Hosted Connection is provisioned by a DX Partner (50 Mbps–10 Gbps), the partner owns the port. <br><br> *Dedicated connections require a Letter of Authorization (LOA-CFA) from AWS for the physical cross-connect at the colocation facility, and setup takes weeks to months.<br> *Hosted connections are faster to provision since the partner already has the physical infrastructure; they support lower bandwidths (down to 50 Mbps) which Dedicated does not offer.<br> *For sub-1Gbps requirements, Hosted is the only option; for 100 Gbps, only Dedicated is available.<br>

10. [ Direct Connect ] A company needs to migrate 500 TB of data from on-premises to S3 as quickly as possible. Direct Connect is already established. What should they use? ; Use AWS DataSync over the existing Direct Connect connection with a Public VIF to transfer data directly to S3. <br><br> *DataSync is optimized for bulk data transfer and can use Direct Connect for private, high-bandwidth transfer directly to S3.<br> *Using DX avoids internet bottlenecks and data transfer charges at internet rates; DX data transfer OUT rates are significantly lower.<br> *For very large datasets (petabytes), AWS Snowball/Snowmobile might be faster than even a 10Gbps DX connection, depending on the timeline required.<br>

11. [ Direct Connect ] What AWS service is the Azure ExpressRoute equivalent? ; AWS Direct Connect. <br><br> *Both AWS Direct Connect and Azure ExpressRoute provide dedicated private fiber connectivity that bypasses the public internet to their respective cloud platforms.<br> *Both support up to 100 Gbps (ExpressRoute Direct / DX Dedicated 100G), Layer 3 BGP routing, and optional MACsec encryption for Layer 2 security.<br> *Key difference: Azure requires redundant ExpressRoute circuits as a best practice; AWS Direct Connect offers an explicit Maximum Resiliency model with detailed SLA tiers.<br>

12. [ Direct Connect ] A company has Direct Connect but wants to avoid paying for a second DX connection as backup. What is the most cost-effective resiliency option? ; Set up a Site-to-Site VPN as a backup to the Direct Connect connection, configured with BGP so VPN takes over automatically when DX fails. <br><br> *Site-to-Site VPN is significantly cheaper than a second DX connection and provides automatic failover via BGP when DX goes down.<br> *The VPN serves as the tertiary path: DX is primary (lower MED/AS path), VPN is secondary, activated automatically by BGP convergence on DX failure.<br> *This is AWS's recommended cost-effective resiliency pattern for non-maximum-resiliency scenarios; maximum resiliency requires 4 DX connections.<br>

13. [ Direct Connect ] MACsec encryption on Direct Connect is available on which connection types? ; Only on Dedicated Direct Connect connections at 10 Gbps and 100 Gbps. <br><br> *MACsec (IEEE 802.1AE) provides Layer 2 hop-by-hop encryption and is only supported on Dedicated connections at 10G and 100G speeds.<br> *Hosted connections and 1 Gbps Dedicated connections do not support MACsec; for encryption on those, use IPSec VPN over a Public VIF.<br> *MACsec encrypts data before it enters the AWS network, providing the highest level of data-in-transit security for Direct Connect.<br>

14. [ Direct Connect ] A company uses AWS Organizations with 50 accounts. They have a single Direct Connect connection and want all accounts to use it. What is the most scalable approach? ; Use AWS Resource Access Manager (RAM) to share the Direct Connect Gateway with all accounts in the organization, and use Transit Gateway in each account or a central Transit Gateway. <br><br> *Direct Connect Gateway can be shared via RAM across an AWS Organization, allowing all member accounts to use the single DX connection without separate physical connections.<br> *A central Transit Gateway associated with the DX Gateway enables the hub-and-spoke model where all spoke VPCs across accounts reach on-premises via the single DX.<br> *This is the standard enterprise pattern for centralized network connectivity: one DX, one DX Gateway, one central TGW, many spoke VPCs across many accounts.<br>

15. [ Direct Connect ] What is the maximum number of VGWs that can be associated with a single Direct Connect Gateway? ; 10 VGWs (Virtual Private Gateways). <br><br> *A single Direct Connect Gateway supports associations with up to 10 Virtual Private Gateways or 3 Transit Gateways.<br> *This limit is important when planning multi-VPC, multi-region connectivity via a single DX connection.<br> *If you need more than 10 VGWs, use Transit Gateway instead (1 TGW can serve many VPCs) and attach the TGW to the DX Gateway via Transit VIF.<br>

16. [ Subnets ] A /26 subnet is created in a VPC. How many usable IP addresses are available for EC2 instances? ; 59 usable IPs (64 total − 5 AWS reserved). <br><br> *A /26 subnet contains 2^(32-26) = 64 IP addresses total.<br> *AWS reserves 5 IP addresses in every subnet: network address, VPC router, DNS (VPC+2), future use, and broadcast address.<br> *64 − 5 = 59 usable IP addresses available for EC2 instances and other resources.<br>

17. [ Subnets ] What defines a public subnet in AWS? ; A public subnet is any subnet that has a route to an Internet Gateway (IGW) in its associated route table. <br><br> *The presence or absence of public IP addresses does NOT define a subnet as public — the route table entry pointing to an IGW is the defining characteristic.<br> *Removing the IGW route from a subnet's route table immediately makes it private, regardless of what IPs the instances have.<br> *For instances in a public subnet to be reachable from the internet, they must also have a public IP or Elastic IP address.<br>

18. [ Subnets ] A company deploying a 3-tier web application wants maximum availability. How many subnets should be created minimum? ; At minimum 6 subnets: 2 public (ALB), 2 private-app (EC2), 2 private-data (RDS) — 2 per tier across 2 AZs. <br><br> *Each subnet exists in exactly one AZ; for high availability you need at least 2 AZs, meaning minimum 2 subnets per tier.<br> *The web tier (public) hosts the load balancer, app tier (private) hosts application servers, data tier (private-isolated) hosts RDS — these tiers should be in separate subnets for security group and NACL isolation.<br> *Using 3 AZs (6 subnets per tier = 9 total for 3-tier) is the production recommendation for AWS workloads.<br>

19. [ Subnets ] Which 5 IP addresses does AWS reserve in a subnet with CIDR 192.168.1.0/24? ; 192.168.1.0 (network), 192.168.1.1 (router), 192.168.1.2 (DNS), 192.168.1.3 (future use), 192.168.1.255 (broadcast). <br><br> *These 5 addresses are reserved in every AWS subnet regardless of size; the first 4 and the last IP are always unavailable.<br> *The .1 address is always the default gateway (VPC router); the .2 address is always the DNS resolver (Amazon DNS = VPC base + 2).<br> *When sizing subnets for EKS or ECS, factor in these reserved addresses plus the high IP consumption of containerized workloads.<br>

20. [ Subnets ] What is the key difference between a Network ACL and a Security Group? ; NACLs are stateless subnet-level firewalls that evaluate rules in number order with explicit allow/deny. Security Groups are stateful instance-level firewalls that allow all return traffic automatically and have allow-only rules. <br><br> *Stateless means NACL must explicitly allow both inbound and outbound traffic including ephemeral return ports (1024-65535); stateful means Security Group automatically allows return traffic for established connections.<br> *NACLs process rules in ascending order by rule number and stop at the first match; Security Groups evaluate ALL rules and take the most permissive outcome.<br> *Use NACLs for broad subnet-level blocking (e.g., deny a known malicious IP range); use Security Groups for precise instance-level access control.<br>

21. [ Subnets ] An EKS cluster with the VPC CNI plugin requires 200 pods. Each node uses instance type m5.large. How many IPs does each m5.large node consume from the subnet? ; Up to 29 IPs per m5.large node (1 primary ENI IP + 3 ENIs × 10 secondary IPs, minus 1 used for the node itself = varies by implementation, check ENI limits for instance type). <br><br> *With VPC CNI, each pod gets a real VPC IP address directly from the subnet, consuming subnet IPs at a much higher rate than traditional networking.<br> *m5.large supports 3 ENIs with 10 IPs each = 30 IPs maximum per node; plan subnet CIDR accordingly for large EKS clusters.<br> *For large EKS clusters, consider /19 or larger subnets, or use custom networking to separate pod IPs from node IPs.<br>

22. [ Subnets ] A company has a VPC with CIDR 10.0.0.0/16. They need to create subnets that will not overlap when peering with another VPC using 10.0.0.0/16. What should they do? ; They cannot peer these two VPCs because the CIDR blocks overlap. Choose non-overlapping CIDRs before deployment — this cannot be fixed after VPC creation. <br><br> *VPC peering requires non-overlapping IPv4 CIDR blocks; two VPCs with the same or overlapping CIDRs cannot be peered.<br> *This is a critical design constraint — CIDR planning must happen before deploying any VPCs, especially in multi-account organizations where future peering is likely.<br> *Best practice: use separate CIDR ranges per account/region, e.g., 10.0.0.0/8 divided by region and account to ensure no overlap for future connectivity.<br>

23. [ Subnets ] What is the smallest subnet size allowed in AWS? ; /28, which provides 16 total IPs (11 usable after AWS reserves 5). <br><br> *AWS supports subnet sizes from /16 (65,536 IPs) down to /28 (16 IPs); anything smaller is not allowed.<br> *A /28 is useful for very small deployments like NAT Gateway subnets (which only need 1 IP) or VPN endpoint subnets.<br> *Even for small workloads, prefer /24 or larger to allow future growth without recreating subnets.<br>

24. [ Subnets ] Instances in a private subnet can reach S3 without a NAT Gateway when a VPC Endpoint is configured. What type of endpoint is used and what does it add to the routing? ; A Gateway Endpoint is used. It adds a route entry to the subnet's route table with a prefix list (pl-xxxxxxxx) pointing to the endpoint. <br><br> *S3 and DynamoDB are the only services supporting Gateway Endpoints, which are free and add a route table entry rather than an ENI.<br> *No NAT Gateway charges for S3/DynamoDB traffic when using Gateway Endpoints — this can significantly reduce costs for high S3-transfer workloads.<br> *The prefix list route takes precedence over the 0.0.0.0/0 NAT GW route for S3/DynamoDB traffic due to more-specific routing.<br>

25. [ Subnets ] A company has resources in two private subnets in different AZs. They have one NAT Gateway in one AZ. What happens if that AZ fails? ; Instances in the private subnet in the surviving AZ lose internet connectivity because their route table still points to the NAT Gateway in the failed AZ. <br><br> *NAT Gateway is an AZ-scoped resource; when its AZ fails, the NAT GW becomes unavailable and any route table pointing to it stops working.<br> *Best practice is to deploy one NAT Gateway per AZ and configure each private subnet's route table to use the NAT GW in the same AZ.<br> *This adds cost (multiple NAT GW hourly charges) but eliminates AZ as a single point of failure and avoids cross-AZ data transfer charges ($0.01/GB each direction).<br>

26. [ Internet Gateway ] What is the bandwidth limit of an AWS Internet Gateway? ; There is no bandwidth limit — the Internet Gateway scales automatically and is not a bottleneck. The bottleneck is the instance's network capacity. <br><br> *IGW is a horizontally scaled, redundant, highly available AWS-managed component with no throughput limits.<br> *There is no hourly charge for an IGW; charges come from the data transfer at the VPC level (data out to internet).<br> *Only one IGW can be attached to a VPC at a time, and one IGW can be detached and reattached to another VPC.<br>

27. [ Internet Gateway ] A company wants instances in private subnets to download OS updates from the internet. What should they use? ; A NAT Gateway deployed in a public subnet, with the private subnet route table pointing 0.0.0.0/0 to the NAT Gateway. <br><br> *NAT Gateway allows private instances to initiate outbound internet connections (like downloading packages) while blocking all unsolicited inbound connections from the internet.<br> *NAT Gateway must be placed in a public subnet (which has an IGW route) and must have an Elastic IP address.<br> *NAT Instance is the legacy alternative but requires self-management, source/destination check disabling, and is limited by EC2 instance type bandwidth.<br>

28. [ Internet Gateway ] What is the key difference between a NAT Gateway and an Egress-Only Internet Gateway? ; NAT Gateway handles IPv4 traffic for private subnets. Egress-Only Internet Gateway handles IPv6 traffic for private subnets. NAT Gateway costs money; Egress-Only IGW is free. <br><br> *IPv6 addresses are globally unique and routable by default, so IPv6 instances in private subnets would be directly reachable from the internet via a regular IGW — Egress-Only IGW prevents this.<br> *Egress-Only IGW allows outbound IPv6 connections and return traffic but blocks all inbound-initiated IPv6 connections, mirroring NAT Gateway behavior for IPv4.<br> *There is no hourly or data processing charge for Egress-Only Internet Gateway, unlike NAT Gateway which charges $0.045/hr + $0.045/GB.<br>

29. [ Internet Gateway ] A company wants to access Amazon S3 from EC2 instances in private subnets without any internet routing and at no additional cost. What should they use? ; A VPC Gateway Endpoint for S3. <br><br> *Gateway Endpoints for S3 and DynamoDB are completely free and route traffic through the AWS private network, never touching the internet.<br> *This also eliminates NAT Gateway data processing charges for all S3 traffic, which can be a significant cost saving for data-intensive workloads.<br> *Gateway Endpoints work by adding a prefix list route to your route table; Interface Endpoints create an ENI and cost per hour and per GB processed.<br>

30. [ Internet Gateway ] What is AWS PrivateLink and when should it be used instead of VPC Peering? ; AWS PrivateLink enables one-way private connectivity from a consumer VPC to a service in a provider VPC via an Interface Endpoint and NLB, without exposing the provider's entire VPC. Use it when you have overlapping CIDRs, need to expose a service to many consumers, or want unidirectional access. <br><br> *VPC Peering requires non-overlapping CIDRs and provides full bidirectional routing between two VPCs; PrivateLink works regardless of CIDR overlap.<br> *With PrivateLink, the consumer never sees the provider's VPC routing — they only access the specific endpoint service, making it more secure for SaaS architectures.<br> *PrivateLink is the technology behind Interface VPC Endpoints; you can also use it to expose your own services (via NLB) to other customers or internal teams.<br>

31. [ Internet Gateway ] A company has 20 VPCs all needing internet access. They currently pay for 20 NAT Gateways. How can they reduce costs? ; Implement a centralized egress pattern using Transit Gateway: route all VPC internet traffic through one shared Egress VPC with a single NAT Gateway. <br><br> *By routing all internet-bound traffic via TGW to a central Egress VPC, you replace 20 NAT Gateways with one (or one per AZ in the egress VPC).<br> *TGW adds per-attachment ($0.05/hr) and per-GB ($0.02/GB) charges, so the break-even depends on traffic volume and number of VPCs — generally cost-effective for 5+ VPCs.<br> *The central egress VPC also provides a single point for outbound internet security inspection (via firewall appliances) and consistent source IP (EIP) for egress traffic.<br>

32. [ Internet Gateway ] Which VPC Endpoint type supports the most AWS services? ; Interface Endpoints (powered by PrivateLink) — they support 100+ AWS services and partner services. <br><br> *Gateway Endpoints only support Amazon S3 and DynamoDB; Interface Endpoints support nearly all other AWS services including SQS, SNS, EC2 API, SSM, Secrets Manager, etc.<br> *Interface Endpoints create an ENI in your subnet with a private IP address; enabling Private DNS causes the standard service FQDN to resolve to this private IP.<br> *Interface Endpoints cost $0.01/hr per AZ and $0.01/GB processed; Gateway Endpoints are free — always use Gateway Endpoints for S3 and DynamoDB.<br>

33. [ Internet Gateway ] A security requirement mandates that all EC2 instances must access AWS Systems Manager (SSM) without any internet connectivity. What endpoints are required? ; Create Interface Endpoints for: com.amazonaws.region.ssm, com.amazonaws.region.ssmmessages, com.amazonaws.region.ec2messages. <br><br> *SSM requires three separate VPC Interface Endpoints to function completely without internet access: ssm (main service), ssmmessages (Session Manager), and ec2messages (EC2 message delivery).<br> *Without these endpoints, SSM agents on instances in private subnets must use NAT Gateway or internet access to reach SSM — which violates the no-internet requirement.<br> *This is a common SAP-C02 exam scenario: replacing internet/NAT dependencies with VPC Endpoints for compliance-driven architectures.<br>

34. [ Internet Gateway ] What happens to internet connectivity when an Internet Gateway is detached from a VPC that has running EC2 instances with public IPs? ; All instances lose internet connectivity immediately. Inbound connections from the internet are dropped. Outbound internet connections from instances in public subnets fail. <br><br> *Detaching the IGW removes the internet route but does not remove the route table entries — they simply fail to route because the target (IGW) no longer exists.<br> *The instances retain their public/Elastic IP addresses, but without an IGW, these IPs are not reachable from the internet or usable for outbound internet traffic.<br> *This is reversible — reattach the IGW (or attach a new one) to restore connectivity; the existing route table entries pointing to the IGW will work again.<br>

35. [ Internet Gateway ] A company needs consistent source IP addresses for all internet-bound traffic from private subnets across multiple AZs. What is the recommended approach? ; Deploy NAT Gateway in each AZ with an Elastic IP address; all traffic from private subnets in that AZ will appear to come from that NAT GW's EIP. <br><br> *Each NAT Gateway has its own Elastic IP, so if you have one NAT GW per AZ, outbound internet traffic has up to 3 source IPs (one per AZ's NAT GW).<br> *If a single consistent source IP is required across all AZs, use a centralized egress VPC via TGW with a single NAT Gateway — all traffic egresses from one EIP.<br> *Elastic IPs on NAT Gateways can be whitelisted by external services; this is a common use case for partner API integrations that require IP whitelisting.<br>

36. [ Direct Connect ] A company's Direct Connect connection experiences high data transfer costs. They primarily transfer data to Amazon S3. How can they reduce costs while keeping DX? ; Create a Public VIF to access S3 via AWS public endpoints over DX. Data transfer over DX has lower rates than internet. Additionally, consider a Gateway Endpoint for S3 in the VPC to avoid VIF data charges for VPC-to-S3 transfers. <br><br> *Data transfer over Direct Connect to S3 is billed at DX data transfer rates, which are lower than standard internet transfer rates.<br> *Using a VPC Gateway Endpoint for S3 (private VIF traffic stays on private IP) can further reduce or eliminate certain data transfer charges.<br> *Always analyze DX vs internet data transfer pricing for your specific transfer volumes; DX typically breaks even around 1+ TB/month of sustained transfer.<br>

37. [ Subnets ] A VPC has CIDR 10.0.0.0/16. An administrator wants to create 8 equally-sized subnets. What subnet mask should be used, and how many usable IPs will each have? ; Use /19 subnet mask (8 subnets per /16). Each /19 has 8,192 IPs − 5 reserved = 8,187 usable IPs. <br><br> *A /16 = 65,536 IPs. Dividing by 8 = 8,192 IPs per subnet = /19 subnet mask (2^(32-19) = 8,192).<br> *The 8 subnets would be: 10.0.0.0/19, 10.0.32.0/19, 10.0.64.0/19, 10.0.96.0/19, 10.0.128.0/19, 10.0.160.0/19, 10.0.192.0/19, 10.0.224.0/19.<br> *Each subnet has 8,192 − 5 = 8,187 usable IP addresses for EC2 instances and other resources.<br>

38. [ Internet Gateway ] Why can't two Internet Gateways be attached to the same VPC simultaneously? ; AWS enforces a hard limit of one IGW per VPC. Each VPC has a single logical internet on/off switch via the IGW attachment. <br><br> *Unlike NAT Gateways (where you can have one per AZ), there is only one IGW per VPC — it is a VPC-level resource, not a subnet or AZ-level resource.<br> *IGW scales automatically and has no throughput limits, so multiple IGWs would provide no additional capacity benefit.<br> *If you need multiple internet entry points (e.g., for different security zones), use multiple VPCs each with their own IGW, connected via Transit Gateway.<br>

39. [ Direct Connect ] What protocol does Direct Connect use for routing between on-premises routers and AWS? ; BGP (Border Gateway Protocol), specifically eBGP (external BGP). <br><br> *BGP is mandatory for all Direct Connect virtual interfaces; static routing is not an option for DX.<br> *You must configure a BGP ASN (Autonomous System Number) on your customer gateway router; you can use a private ASN (64512-65534) if you don't have a public one.<br> *BGP handles automatic failover: when a DX connection fails, BGP withdraws the routes and traffic shifts to backup paths (VPN or second DX) within BGP convergence time.<br>

40. [ Internet Gateway ] A workload in a private subnet needs to access a partner API that whitelists specific IP addresses. How should this be designed? ; Deploy a NAT Gateway with an Elastic IP in a public subnet. Configure the private subnet route table to use the NAT Gateway. Provide the NAT Gateway's EIP to the partner for whitelisting. <br><br> *NAT Gateway's Elastic IP appears as the source IP for all outbound internet traffic from private subnets, providing a stable, predictable IP for partner whitelisting.<br> *Without NAT Gateway, instances in private subnets have no public IP and cannot initiate connections to the internet.<br> *Elastic IPs remain stable until explicitly released; NAT Gateway EIPs can be used for long-term whitelisting relationships with external partners.<br>

41. [ Direct Connect ] A company currently uses IPSec VPN to connect on-premises to AWS. They want to migrate to Direct Connect but maintain the VPN as backup. What is the recommended migration sequence? ; 1) Order DX and wait for provisioning. 2) Configure DX with Private VIF and establish BGP. 3) Set DX as preferred path via MED. 4) Test DX connectivity. 5) Keep VPN as backup with higher MED/AS prepending. Do NOT remove VPN until DX is fully validated. <br><br> *Running both DX and VPN simultaneously during migration ensures zero-downtime transition; BGP preference values control which path is used.<br> *The VPN remains as automatic failback — if DX BGP session drops, VPN routes are already advertised and traffic fails over automatically.<br> *Only decommission the VPN after DX has been stable for a validation period and all stakeholders agree the migration is complete.<br>

42. [ Subnets ] A NACL rule is configured as Rule 100: Allow HTTP inbound. What additional rule is required for web servers in the subnet to respond to clients? ; Add an outbound rule allowing ephemeral ports (1024-65535) to the client IP ranges, because NACLs are stateless. <br><br> *NACLs are stateless — unlike Security Groups, they do not automatically allow return traffic for established connections.<br> *HTTP responses use ephemeral ports (the client picks a random high port for the return traffic), so you must explicitly allow outbound traffic on ports 1024-65535.<br> *Forgetting the ephemeral port outbound rule is a classic NACL misconfiguration that causes web servers to appear unreachable even when inbound is correctly configured.<br>

43. [ Internet Gateway ] What is the maximum number of VPC Interface Endpoints that can be created per VPC by default? ; 50 per VPC (soft limit, can be increased via Support). <br><br> *Interface Endpoints are AZ-specific ENIs; creating one endpoint for a service in 3 AZs uses 3 of your 50 endpoint slots.<br> *For large organizations with many services using Interface Endpoints, share endpoints across VPCs using AWS PrivateLink or centralize endpoints in a shared VPC accessible via TGW.<br> *Gateway Endpoints (S3 and DynamoDB) do not count against this limit — they are route table entries, not ENIs.<br>

44. [ Direct Connect ] A financial services company requires Layer 2 encryption on their Direct Connect connection. Which feature provides this and what are the requirements? ; MACsec (IEEE 802.1AE) provides Layer 2 encryption on Direct Connect. Requirements: Dedicated connection only, 10 Gbps or 100 Gbps speed, and MACsec-capable router on the customer side. <br><br> *MACsec encrypts traffic between your router and the AWS DX router at the colocation facility, before data enters the AWS network — this is the earliest possible encryption point.<br> *MACsec is not available on Hosted connections or 1 Gbps Dedicated connections — only 10G/100G Dedicated connections support it.<br> *For connections that don't support MACsec, the alternative is IPSec VPN over a Public VIF, which provides encryption at Layer 3 but adds overhead and latency.<br>

45. [ Internet Gateway ] A company runs a three-tier application (public ALB, private app servers, private RDS). The RDS must never have internet access even accidentally. What subnet configuration enforces this? ; Place RDS in an "isolated" private subnet whose route table has ONLY the local VPC route (10.0.0.0/16 → local) and no 0.0.0.0/0 route to NAT GW or IGW. <br><br> *An isolated subnet has no route to the internet — not even via NAT Gateway — making it impossible for RDS to initiate or receive internet connections.<br> *App servers need outbound internet access (e.g., for OS updates) so they go in a "private" subnet with NAT GW route; databases don't need internet so they go in truly isolated subnets.<br> *Additional defense: apply strict Security Group rules to RDS allowing only traffic from the app server Security Group on the database port (e.g., 5432 for PostgreSQL).<br>

46. [ Direct Connect ] What is a Transit VIF and when is it used instead of a Private VIF? ; A Transit VIF connects Direct Connect to a Transit Gateway (via DX Gateway). Use it when you need to connect to multiple VPCs or when VPCs are connected via Transit Gateway rather than individual VGWs. <br><br> *Private VIF connects to a Virtual Private Gateway (one VIF per VGW = one VIF per VPC directly attached), which doesn't scale beyond 10 VPCs per DX Gateway.<br> *Transit VIF connects to a TGW via DX Gateway; since TGW can handle thousands of VPC attachments, this scales to large enterprise environments.<br> *You cannot use both Private VIF and Transit VIF in the same DX Gateway for the same on-premises router — choose one model per network design.<br>

47. [ Subnets ] Can IPv6 subnets use a NAT Gateway for outbound internet access? ; No. NAT Gateway only supports IPv4. IPv6 subnets use an Egress-Only Internet Gateway for outbound-only access. <br><br> *IPv6 addresses are globally routable and don't require NAT — the Egress-Only IGW provides stateful tracking to block inbound-initiated connections without address translation.<br> *If you add an IGW route to an IPv6 subnet's route table (with IPv6 CIDR), instances with IPv6 addresses become reachable from the internet — to prevent this while allowing outbound, use Egress-Only IGW.<br> *IPv4 and IPv6 egress paths are separate: IPv4 private subnets use NAT GW; IPv6 private subnets use Egress-Only IGW; both can coexist in a dual-stack subnet.<br>

48. [ Internet Gateway ] A company wants to allow third-party vendors to access a specific microservice in their VPC without exposing the entire VPC or requiring VPN. What AWS service enables this? ; AWS PrivateLink — expose the microservice behind a Network Load Balancer as a VPC Endpoint Service; vendors create Interface Endpoints in their VPCs to consume it. <br><br> *PrivateLink provides one-way, service-specific connectivity: vendors can only reach the NLB endpoint, never the broader VPC network.<br> *Traffic stays on the AWS private network and never traverses the internet; overlapping CIDRs between vendor and provider VPC are not a problem with PrivateLink.<br> *The provider controls access via an allow-list of AWS account IDs or IAM principals that can create endpoints to the service.<br>

49. [ Direct Connect ] What is the recommended Direct Connect resiliency model for a production financial services application? ; Maximum Resiliency: two physical connections at two separate DX locations, each terminating on separate AWS devices. This provides redundancy against device failure, cable failure, and entire location failure. <br><br> *Maximum Resiliency is the only model that protects against all single points of failure: per-device, per-location, and per-physical-path failures.<br> *High Resiliency (two locations, one device each) still fails if both locations fail simultaneously — extremely rare but not zero risk for maximum-uptime requirements.<br> *AWS provides a 99.99% SLA only for the Maximum Resiliency configuration; other models have lower SLAs or no SLA at all.<br>

50. [ Internet Gateway ] What is the purpose of disabling the source/destination check on a NAT Instance (EC2-based NAT)? ; Because a NAT instance forwards traffic that is neither sourced from nor destined to its own IP address. AWS by default drops such traffic; disabling the check allows the NAT instance to forward packets on behalf of other hosts. <br><br> *EC2 instances normally only send/receive traffic with their own IP as source/destination; any other traffic is dropped by the hypervisor-level source/dest check.<br> *NAT instances act as routers — they receive packets from private subnet instances (different source IPs) and forward them to the internet (different destination IPs), so the check must be disabled.<br> *NAT Gateways (managed service) automatically handle this; it's only a manual step required for NAT instances, which is one reason NAT Gateways are preferred.<br>

51. [ Direct Connect ] A company wants to migrate 1 PB of data from on-premises to S3 within 2 weeks. They have a 10 Gbps Direct Connect. Is DX sufficient? ; No. 10 Gbps DX can transfer ~1 PB in approximately 9.2 days at 100% utilization (10 Gbps × 86400 sec × 14 days = ~1.2 PB theoretical max). However, real-world utilization is 40-60%, making it likely insufficient. Use AWS Snowball Edge or Snowmobile for this scale. <br><br> *At 100% utilization (unrealistic), 10 Gbps = 1.25 GB/s × 86400 s/day = 108 TB/day × 9.26 days = 1 PB. Realistic utilization (50%) = ~18.5 days.<br> *AWS Snowball Edge (80 TB per device) would require ~13 devices; AWS Snowmobile handles up to 100 PB in a single truck for very large migrations.<br> *For time-sensitive petabyte-scale migrations, physical data transfer devices are faster and often cheaper than even dedicated high-bandwidth DX connections.<br>

52. [ Subnets ] A company uses Shared VPC (AWS RAM). Account A owns the VPC and subnets. Account B deploys EC2 instances in the shared subnet. Where should Security Groups be created? ; Each account manages Security Groups for its own resources in the shared VPC. Account A's SGs and Account B's SGs can reference each other, but Account B cannot modify Account A's SGs. <br><br> *In Shared VPC, the subnet owner (Account A) controls the VPC, subnets, and route tables. Participant accounts (Account B) create their own SGs within the shared VPC.<br> *SGs from different accounts in the same shared VPC can reference each other by SG ID, enabling cross-account security group rules without peering.<br> *NACLs are owned and managed by the VPC owner (Account A); participant accounts cannot modify NACLs but are subject to them.<br>

53. [ Internet Gateway ] What is the data processing charge for a VPC Gateway Endpoint for S3? ; Zero — Gateway Endpoints have no data processing or hourly charges. They are completely free. <br><br> *Gateway Endpoints (S3 and DynamoDB only) are free: no per-hour charge, no per-GB charge, and no NAT Gateway data processing fees for traffic that uses the endpoint.<br> *Interface Endpoints cost $0.01/hour per AZ and $0.01/GB of data processed — always prefer Gateway Endpoints for S3 and DynamoDB to avoid these costs.<br> *The only way to access S3 and DynamoDB privately for free is via Gateway Endpoints; Interface Endpoints for S3 also exist but are chargeable (used for on-premises access via DX/VPN).<br>

54. [ Direct Connect ] How does BGP MED influence routing when a company has both Direct Connect and VPN to the same VPC? ; Lower MED value = more preferred path. Set MED=100 on DX BGP advertisements (preferred) and MED=200 on VPN BGP advertisements (backup). AWS will prefer the DX path when selecting between equal-cost paths. <br><br> *MED is a BGP attribute exchanged between neighboring ASes to indicate the preferred entry point into your AS from AWS's perspective.<br> *AWS honors MED within the same AWS ASN; if routes arrive via different AWS ASNs, MED comparisons are not made across ASN boundaries.<br> *When DX fails and the BGP session drops, DX routes are withdrawn and AWS automatically falls back to VPN routes without manual intervention.<br>

55. [ Subnets ] What is the difference between a default VPC and a non-default VPC in terms of subnets? ; Default VPC has a /20 public subnet per AZ with auto-assign public IPs enabled and an IGW attached. Non-default VPC has no subnets, no IGW, and instances don't get public IPs by default. <br><br> *The default VPC is created automatically in each AWS Region when an account is created; it's designed for easy use but not for production security-sensitive workloads.<br> *In default VPC subnets, instances launched without specifying a subnet automatically get both public and private IP addresses — this is a security risk for production environments.<br> *Best practice: do not use the default VPC for production workloads; create custom VPCs with explicit subnet types, route tables, and security controls.<br>

56. [ Internet Gateway ] Can a VPC Interface Endpoint be accessed from on-premises over Direct Connect? ; Yes — when Private DNS is enabled for the Interface Endpoint, DNS resolves the service FQDN to the ENI's private IP. Traffic from on-premises (via DX Private VIF) routes to that private IP through the VPC. <br><br> *This is a key hybrid connectivity pattern: instead of accessing AWS services via the internet, on-premises systems use DX + Interface Endpoints to keep all traffic private.<br> *For S3 access from on-premises privately, use an Interface Endpoint for S3 (not Gateway Endpoint, which only works from within the VPC via route table).<br> *DNS resolution from on-premises must resolve the AWS service FQDN to the Interface Endpoint's private IP — this requires Route 53 Resolver or custom DNS forwarding.<br>

57. [ Direct Connect ] A company receives a notification that their Direct Connect connection will have maintenance downtime in 48 hours. What should be the immediate response? ; Verify that the backup path (VPN or second DX) is functioning correctly, test failover, and pre-position BGP to use the backup path during the maintenance window. <br><br> *Planned DX maintenance windows are announced in advance via the AWS Personal Health Dashboard; having a tested backup path eliminates maintenance downtime.<br> *Test the failover by manually preferring the backup path (temporarily adjust MED or AS prepend) before the maintenance window to verify the backup works under load.<br> *After maintenance completes and DX is back up, restore original BGP preferences to return DX as the primary path.<br>

58. [ Internet Gateway ] A company has a Lambda function in a VPC that needs to access both the internet and an internal RDS database. What network configuration is required? ; Lambda must be in a private subnet. Deploy a NAT Gateway in a public subnet. Lambda's subnet route table: local route for RDS, 0.0.0.0/0 to NAT GW. RDS in isolated subnet accessible via SG rule allowing Lambda's SG. <br><br> *Lambda functions placed in a VPC use ENIs in the specified subnets and follow normal VPC routing rules — they do not have automatic internet access.<br> *Lambda in a VPC needs NAT GW for internet access; Lambda NOT in a VPC has automatic internet access but cannot reach private VPC resources like RDS.<br> *You cannot place Lambda in a public subnet and expect it to get internet access via IGW — Lambda ENIs never have public IPs assigned, so IGW NAT doesn't work for Lambda.<br>

59. [ Subnets ] Why is CIDR planning critical in AWS Organizations with many accounts? ; VPC peering and Transit Gateway require non-overlapping CIDRs. Without pre-planned CIDR ranges per account/region, future connectivity between accounts becomes impossible or requires expensive re-IPing. <br><br> *Once a VPC is created with a CIDR, it cannot be changed; you can add secondary CIDRs but cannot remove the primary one.<br> *A common enterprise pattern: allocate a /8 (e.g., 10.0.0.0/8), divide by region (/12 per region), divide by account (/16 per account), divide by environment (/20 per VPC), subnet per AZ per tier (/24).<br> *Overlapping CIDRs are the most common blocker for implementing Transit Gateway or VPC Peering in organizations that grew without a CIDR plan.<br>

60. [ Internet Gateway ] What is the difference between VPC Endpoint Policies and Security Groups on Interface Endpoints? ; Endpoint Policies are IAM-based resource policies attached to the endpoint that control which principals and actions are allowed (e.g., restrict S3 access to specific buckets). Security Groups on Interface Endpoints control network-level access (which IPs/SGs can reach the endpoint ENI). <br><br> *Endpoint policies provide IAM-layer control: you can restrict an S3 endpoint to only allow GetObject on a specific bucket, preventing access to other S3 buckets via that endpoint.<br> *Security Groups on Interface Endpoints control TCP/IP access: only sources in specified SGs or CIDR ranges can establish connections to the endpoint ENI on the service port.<br> *Both controls are additive for defense-in-depth: network SG controls who can reach the endpoint, IAM endpoint policy controls what they can do through it.<br>

61. [ Direct Connect ] What is the significance of the AWS DX location (Point of Presence) in Direct Connect architecture? ; The DX location is the physical colocation facility (e.g., Equinix, CoreSite) where your router must establish a cross-connect with AWS's DX router. It is the physical meeting point between your network and AWS. <br><br> *You or your network provider must have equipment (or arrange a partner connection) at one of the ~100 DX locations worldwide; you cannot create a DX connection without this physical presence.<br> *The DX location is distinct from the AWS Region — one location may serve multiple Regions, and one Region may be served by multiple locations.<br> *For Maximum Resiliency, choose two DX locations that are in different physical buildings (not just different floors of the same building) and verified to use separate power/cooling infrastructure.<br>

62. [ Internet Gateway ] A company needs to restrict S3 access so that only traffic from their specific VPC can access a particular S3 bucket. What combination of controls achieves this? ; Use an S3 bucket policy with aws:sourceVpc condition key specifying the VPC ID, combined with a VPC Gateway Endpoint for S3. <br><br> *The S3 bucket policy with `aws:sourceVpc: vpc-xxxxxxxx` allows access only when requests originate from that specific VPC, blocking all access from the internet or other VPCs.<br> *The Gateway Endpoint ensures traffic from the VPC uses the private AWS path and carries the VPC identity in the request context for the bucket policy evaluation.<br> *This pattern is commonly used for compliant data lakes and sensitive S3 buckets that must never be accessed outside the corporate VPC.<br>

63. [ Subnets ] What routing change occurs in a subnet's route table when a VPC Gateway Endpoint for S3 is created? ; A new route is added with the S3 prefix list (pl-xxxxxxxxx) as the destination and the VPC Endpoint ID (vpce-xxx) as the target. <br><br> *The prefix list contains all S3 IP ranges for the region and is managed by AWS (automatically updated when S3 IP ranges change).<br> *This route takes precedence over the broader 0.0.0.0/0 → NAT GW route due to more-specific prefix matching, so S3 traffic bypasses NAT Gateway automatically.<br> *If you remove the endpoint but don't remove the route, the route points to a non-existent endpoint and S3 traffic fails — always clean up route table entries when deleting endpoints.<br>

64. [ Direct Connect ] A company needs on-premises servers to access AWS public services (CloudFront, EC2 endpoints, Route 53) privately via Direct Connect without using the public internet. Which VIF type enables this? ; A Public Virtual Interface (Public VIF) allows on-premises access to all AWS public IP addresses via the Direct Connect private path. <br><br> *Public VIF gives you access to AWS public endpoints over the DX connection, avoiding the public internet while still reaching services that have public IPs.<br> *With a Public VIF, AWS advertises all AWS public IP prefixes to your on-premises router; your router sends traffic for those prefixes over DX instead of internet.<br> *Public VIF does NOT give you access to your VPC's private resources — for that, use a Private VIF or Transit VIF.<br>

65. [ Internet Gateway ] What happens to NAT Gateway connectivity if the Elastic IP associated with it is disassociated? ; NAT Gateway stops functioning — it requires an EIP to operate. You must create a new NAT Gateway with a new or different EIP; you cannot reassociate an EIP to an existing NAT Gateway. <br><br> *NAT Gateway EIPs cannot be modified after creation — if you need a different EIP, create a new NAT Gateway, update route tables to point to the new one, then delete the old one.<br> *Disassociating an EIP from a NAT Gateway is not a supported operation — this is different from EC2 instances where EIPs can be associated/disassociated dynamically.<br> *If the EIP is released, all outbound connections from private subnets are lost until a new NAT Gateway is provisioned.<br>

66. [ Subnets ] A company needs to give a partner AWS account access to resources in their VPC's private subnet without VPC peering. What is the recommended solution? ; Use AWS PrivateLink (Interface Endpoint Service) — expose the service via a Network Load Balancer, allow the partner's account to create an Interface Endpoint in their VPC to consume it. <br><br> *PrivateLink provides targeted, one-way service exposure: the partner can only reach the specific service behind the NLB, never the full VPC.<br> *VPC Peering would give full network access between VPCs (both directions), which is more than required and creates security risk.<br> *PrivateLink works across accounts and even across organizations, with the service provider approving endpoint creation requests.<br>

67. [ Direct Connect ] What is the purpose of BGP AS Path Prepending in a DX + VPN dual-connection setup? ; AS Path Prepending makes a BGP path appear longer (less preferred) by repeating the ASN multiple times in the AS_PATH attribute. Applied to VPN advertisements, it makes AWS prefer the DX path over VPN. <br><br> *BGP path selection prefers shorter AS paths; prepending your ASN (e.g., 65001 65001 65001) to VPN advertisements makes the VPN path 3 hops "longer" than DX.<br> *This influences the outbound direction from AWS to on-premises; MED influences the inbound direction (on-premises to AWS).<br> *Both MED and AS prepending are needed for full traffic engineering: MED controls which path on-premises traffic takes to AWS, AS prepending controls which path AWS traffic takes to on-premises.<br>

68. [ Internet Gateway ] Can an Interface VPC Endpoint be shared across VPCs? ; Not directly — Interface Endpoints are VPC-specific. However, in a central VPC design, an Interface Endpoint in a hub VPC can be accessed from spoke VPCs via Transit Gateway if proper routing and DNS resolution are configured. <br><br> *Sharing endpoints via TGW requires: Interface Endpoint in hub VPC, TGW routing from spoke to hub, and DNS resolution in spoke VPCs pointing to hub endpoint's private IPs.<br> *This centralized endpoint pattern reduces costs significantly for organizations with many VPCs — instead of Interface Endpoints per VPC, one set of endpoints in a central VPC serves all.<br> *AWS RAM cannot directly share Interface Endpoints; the TGW + centralized hub pattern is the standard architecture for sharing.<br>

69. [ Subnets ] An application in a private subnet needs to make API calls to AWS services. The company wants minimum cost. What is the priority order of solutions? ; 1) Gateway Endpoints (S3/DynamoDB - free), 2) VPC Endpoints Interface type (hourly + per GB), 3) NAT Gateway (hourly + per GB, but enables all services). <br><br> *For S3 and DynamoDB, always use free Gateway Endpoints — there is no reason to use NAT GW or Interface Endpoints for these services from within a VPC.<br> *For other services like SQS, SNS, SSM, Secrets Manager — Interface Endpoints avoid NAT GW data charges but add hourly ENI costs; evaluate based on traffic volume.<br> *NAT Gateway is the fallback for services without VPC Endpoints; it enables internet and all AWS public endpoint access but costs per hour and per GB processed.<br>

70. [ Direct Connect ] A company runs a multi-account AWS Organization. They want all accounts to share a single Direct Connect connection via Transit Gateway. How should this be architected? ; 1) Create a Network/Connectivity account with the DX connection and a Transit Gateway. 2) Share the TGW via AWS RAM to all member accounts. 3) Attach each account's VPCs to the shared TGW. 4) Create a Transit VIF from DX Gateway to the TGW. On-premises routes propagate to all TGW attachments. <br><br> *This is the AWS recommended Landing Zone network pattern: centralize network connectivity (DX, TGW, NAT GW, firewalls) in a dedicated Network account.<br> *Transit Gateway route tables control which VPCs can communicate with each other and with on-premises; you can create isolated route tables for regulatory segregation.<br> *The DX Gateway → Transit VIF → TGW chain allows one physical DX connection to serve hundreds of VPCs across dozens of accounts in multiple regions.<br>

71. [ Internet Gateway ] What is the difference between a Public NAT Gateway and a Private NAT Gateway? ; Public NAT Gateway: has an EIP, enables private subnets to access the internet. Private NAT Gateway: no EIP, enables connectivity between VPCs or on-premises networks via Transit Gateway or VPC Peering, without internet access. <br><br> *Private NAT Gateway is used in scenarios where you need to translate overlapping CIDRs — two VPCs with the same CIDR range can communicate via Private NAT GW + TGW.<br> *Public NAT Gateway is the traditional use case for private subnet internet access; Private NAT Gateway is for complex network topologies with overlapping address spaces.<br> *Private NAT Gateway enables non-internet routable connectivity, which is important for compliance scenarios where internet access is prohibited but cross-VPC communication is needed.<br>

72. [ Subnets ] What AWS feature allows multiple accounts to launch resources in the same VPC's subnets? ; VPC Sharing via AWS Resource Access Manager (RAM). The VPC owner account shares specific subnets with other accounts in the same AWS Organization. <br><br> *With shared subnets, participant accounts create their own resources (EC2, ELB, RDS) in the shared subnet but cannot modify the subnet itself or its route table.<br> *This reduces VPC sprawl in large organizations: instead of a VPC per account, teams share infrastructure while maintaining separate billing and IAM boundaries.<br> *NACLs, route tables, and IGW are controlled by the VPC owner; Security Groups are managed per-account within the shared VPC.<br>

73. [ Direct Connect ] What is a Hosted Virtual Interface (Hosted VIF) in Direct Connect? ; A Hosted VIF is a single Virtual Interface provisioned by a Direct Connect Partner on their connection and assigned to your AWS account. You get one VIF (not the physical connection). <br><br> *Hosted VIF differs from Hosted Connection: Hosted Connection gives you dedicated bandwidth on a partner port; Hosted VIF gives you a logical VIF sharing the partner's capacity.<br> *Hosted VIFs can have lower bandwidth than Hosted Connections and may share capacity with other customers on the partner's infrastructure.<br> *Use Hosted VIF for low-cost, quick-to-provision DX access when you don't need dedicated bandwidth; use Dedicated or Hosted Connection for guaranteed throughput.<br>

74. [ Internet Gateway ] A company wants to implement a security inspection layer for all internet-bound traffic from multiple VPCs. What architecture should they use? ; Deploy a centralized Inspection VPC with a network firewall appliance (AWS Network Firewall or third-party). Use Transit Gateway to route all VPC traffic to the Inspection VPC before sending to the internet via NAT Gateway + IGW. <br><br> *TGW with custom route tables directs all internet-bound traffic (0.0.0.0/0) from spoke VPCs to the inspection VPC instead of directly to the internet.<br> *The Inspection VPC hosts AWS Network Firewall or third-party firewalls (Palo Alto, Check Point) behind a Gateway Load Balancer for deep packet inspection.<br> *After inspection, traffic is forwarded to a NAT Gateway in the Inspection VPC's public subnet for internet egress — all egress traffic passes through the centralized security layer.<br>

75. [ Subnets ] An organization discovers their subnets are running out of IP addresses. Can they resize an existing subnet? ; No. Subnet CIDR blocks cannot be resized or modified after creation. You must create a new subnet with a larger CIDR and migrate resources to it. <br><br> *This is a hard AWS limitation — subnet CIDRs are immutable. However, you can add secondary CIDR blocks to the VPC and create new larger subnets in that secondary CIDR.<br> *For EC2 instances, migration means: launch new instances in the new subnet, update DNS/load balancers, terminate old instances. For stateful services (RDS), use snapshots and restore to a new subnet.<br> *This limitation makes proactive CIDR planning critical — always allocate subnets larger than you think you need; unused IPs cost nothing.<br>

76. [ Direct Connect ] Can Direct Connect Gateway be used to route traffic between two VPCs? ; No. Direct Connect Gateway provides connectivity only from on-premises to VPCs (one direction: customer → VPC). VPC-to-VPC routing is not possible through DX Gateway. <br><br> *DX Gateway is not a VPC peering or transit solution — it is strictly for connecting on-premises networks to AWS VPCs via a Direct Connect connection.<br> *For VPC-to-VPC routing, use Transit Gateway (which can also attach to DX Gateway for on-premises connectivity) or VPC Peering.<br> *This is a common exam trap: DX Gateway looks like it could enable VPC-to-VPC routing, but it explicitly does not support this topology.<br>

77. [ Internet Gateway ] What is the maximum number of NAT Gateways per Availability Zone per VPC? ; One NAT Gateway per public subnet (and you can have multiple public subnets per AZ, so technically multiple per AZ, but best practice is one per AZ). The default limit is 5 per AZ per account (soft limit). <br><br> *While you technically can have multiple NAT Gateways in the same AZ, the standard pattern is one NAT GW per AZ for high availability, each in a separate public subnet.<br> *Having multiple NAT GWs in the same AZ provides no HA benefit (same AZ = same failure domain) but does allow traffic distribution or specific routing for different subnets.<br> *The 5 per AZ default limit can be raised via a Support request if an architecture genuinely requires more.<br>

78. [ Subnets ] A company wants to ensure all traffic between subnets in a VPC is inspected by a security appliance. What architecture enables this? ; Use a Transit Gateway with separate route tables per VPC/subnet, routing all east-west traffic through an Inspection VPC with a security appliance (AWS Network Firewall or Gateway Load Balancer + firewall). <br><br> *By default, traffic between subnets in the same VPC routes locally without inspection; to force inspection, route traffic via TGW to an inspection layer before allowing it to reach the destination.<br> *Gateway Load Balancer (GWLB) distributes traffic to a fleet of firewall appliances transparently (GENEVE protocol, preserving original source/destination IPs).<br> *This architecture adds latency and cost but is required for compliance scenarios (PCI-DSS, financial services) that mandate all east-west traffic inspection.<br>

79. [ Direct Connect ] What happens to Direct Connect connection costs when you use a Hosted Connection from a partner? ; You pay both the AWS Direct Connect port hour fee and the partner's fees for the hosted connection. AWS fees are based on port speed; partner fees vary by provider and bandwidth. <br><br> *For Hosted Connections, AWS bills you directly for the DX port usage; the partner also bills you for the circuit/connectivity service on their end.<br> *This dual-billing model means Hosted Connections can sometimes cost more than Dedicated in total, but they offer lower minimum commitment and faster provisioning.<br> *Always calculate total cost (AWS + partner) when comparing Hosted vs Dedicated connections for large, long-term deployments.<br>

80. [ Internet Gateway ] A company uses NAT Gateway and notices very high data processing charges. Investigation shows 80% of traffic is EC2 instances downloading from S3. What is the most cost-effective fix? ; Create a VPC Gateway Endpoint for S3. All S3 traffic will route through the endpoint (free, no data processing charge) instead of through NAT Gateway. <br><br> *Gateway Endpoints add a route table entry that takes precedence over the NAT GW route for S3 destinations; no code or application changes are needed.<br> *NAT Gateway charges $0.045/GB for data processing; Gateway Endpoint has zero data processing charges — the saving is immediate and requires no ongoing maintenance.<br> *This is one of the most impactful AWS cost optimizations for workloads with heavy S3 access from private subnets — savings can be substantial at scale.<br>

81. [ Direct Connect ] A company wants to maintain separate BGP routing policies for traffic to different VPCs over the same DX connection. How can they achieve this? ; Use separate Private VIFs for each VPC/VGW, each with independent BGP sessions and different route advertisements or MED values. Or use Transit Gateway with TGW route tables for policy-based routing. <br><br> *Each Virtual Interface has its own BGP session with its own set of routes, MED values, and BGP communities — you can have different routing policies per VIF.<br> *With Transit Gateway, TGW route tables allow you to control which attachments (VPCs, DX, VPN) can route to each other and with what priority.<br> *Multiple Private VIFs on the same DX connection allow granular per-VPC routing control without TGW, but at the cost of managing more BGP sessions.<br>

82. [ Internet Gateway ] What is the key advantage of AWS PrivateLink over VPC Peering for accessing services across accounts? ; PrivateLink works with overlapping CIDR blocks (VPC peering does not), provides unidirectional service-level access (not full VPC routing), and scales to many consumers without complex routing. <br><br> *VPC Peering requires non-overlapping CIDRs across all peered VPCs, which becomes impossible to maintain at enterprise scale without strict CIDR planning.<br> *PrivateLink consumers only see the specific service endpoint, not the entire provider VPC — this reduces the blast radius if an account is compromised.<br> *AWS's own services (SQS, SNS, EC2 API, etc.) are consumed via PrivateLink Interface Endpoints — this is the same mechanism you can use for your own services.<br>

83. [ Subnets ] How does an EC2 instance in a public subnet get a public IP address? ; Two ways: 1) Auto-assign public IP enabled on the subnet — instance gets a temporary public IP at launch (released on stop/terminate). 2) Elastic IP manually associated — persistent IP that stays until explicitly disassociated. <br><br> *Auto-assigned public IPs change on every instance stop/start; Elastic IPs remain constant — use EIPs for servers that need a stable public IP (DNS records, partner whitelisting).<br> *Public IPs are not visible inside the instance (ifconfig shows only private IP); the IGW performs the 1:1 NAT translation transparently.<br> *IPv6 addresses (if enabled) are always the actual instance address visible inside the OS — no NAT, no translation, globally routable directly.<br>

84. [ Internet Gateway ] Can a VPN Gateway and an Internet Gateway coexist in the same VPC? ; Yes. A VPC can simultaneously have an IGW (for internet access), a Virtual Private Gateway (for VPN/DX to on-premises), and VPC Endpoints — these are not mutually exclusive. <br><br> *Route tables determine which traffic goes where: local VPC traffic uses the local route, on-premises prefixes route to VGW, internet traffic routes to IGW, AWS service traffic routes to VPC Endpoints.<br> *The most common hybrid architecture uses all three simultaneously: internet-facing ALB via IGW, app servers via NAT GW (also via IGW), on-premises connectivity via VGW.<br> *VGW and TGW are mutually exclusive on the same VPC — you can attach either a VGW or connect the VPC to a TGW, not both for the same routing purpose.<br>

85. [ Direct Connect ] A company needs to access AWS services from on-premises without any traffic traversing the public internet, even for AWS public services like S3. What architecture achieves this? ; Use Direct Connect with a Private VIF for VPC resources + Interface Endpoint for S3 (with Private DNS) in the VPC, accessible from on-premises via DX. For other public services, use Public VIF (routes to AWS public IPs over DX private path). <br><br> *Public VIF over DX routes traffic to AWS public IPs via the DX private connection — the traffic takes the DX path, not the public internet, even though the destination is a public IP.<br> *Interface Endpoint for S3 in the VPC enables on-premises S3 access via DX Private VIF → VPC → Interface Endpoint, keeping all traffic private with bucket-level access control.<br> *This is the fully private hybrid connectivity pattern used by financial and healthcare organizations that prohibit any public internet transit.<br>

86. [ Subnets ] What is the subnet association behavior when no explicit route table is associated with a subnet? ; The subnet automatically uses the VPC's main route table. <br><br> *Every VPC has one main route table; all subnets without an explicit association use this main route table by default.<br> *Best practice: do not modify the main route table — create explicit route tables and associate them to subnets. This prevents accidentally making all subnets public or private.<br> *Changing the main route table affects all subnets that rely on it implicitly, which can cause widespread connectivity issues if done incorrectly.<br>

87. [ Internet Gateway ] What monitoring is available for NAT Gateway to detect issues? ; CloudWatch metrics: ActiveConnectionCount, BytesInFromDestination, BytesOutToDestination, ErrorPortAllocation (port exhaustion), PacketsDropCount, IdleTimeoutCount. <br><br> *ErrorPortAllocation is the most critical metric — it indicates the NAT Gateway has run out of ports (max 55,000 simultaneous connections to same destination IP:port combo) and is dropping connections.<br> *To resolve port exhaustion: distribute connections across multiple NAT Gateways, use connection pooling in applications, or reduce connection rates to the same destination.<br> *VPC Flow Logs at the NAT Gateway ENI level provide detailed connection-level visibility for troubleshooting connectivity issues through NAT.<br>

88. [ Direct Connect ] What is the difference between Direct Connect and Site-to-Site VPN in terms of encryption? ; Direct Connect: no encryption by default (add IPSec VPN over DX or MACsec for encryption). Site-to-Site VPN: always encrypted (IPSec). <br><br> *This means DX alone is not sufficient for workloads requiring encrypted transit; you must explicitly add encryption via MACsec (L2) or IPSec VPN over DX (L3).<br> *Site-to-Site VPN provides encryption by default but uses the public internet for transport, leading to variable latency and limited bandwidth (1.25 Gbps max).<br> *The combination of DX + IPSec VPN (VPN over DX) gives you both consistent performance/latency AND encryption — the recommended pattern for compliance-sensitive hybrid workloads.<br>

89. [ Subnets ] A company wants to prevent resources in a private subnet from being able to reach the internet under any circumstances, including through a misconfigured route table. What is the defense-in-depth approach? ; Use isolated subnets (no 0.0.0.0/0 route), combined with restrictive NACLs blocking all outbound traffic to non-VPC CIDR ranges, and Security Groups with no outbound internet rules. <br><br> *Defense-in-depth: isolated route table (no internet route) + NACL (deny outbound to 0.0.0.0/0) + Security Group (no outbound internet rules) — three layers of control.<br> *Even if someone adds a route to the route table accidentally, the NACL will block the traffic at the subnet level before it can reach the IGW/NAT.<br> *This pattern is mandatory for PCI-DSS and HIPAA workloads where cardholder/health data systems must be provably isolated from internet access.<br>

90. [ Internet Gateway ] What is the maximum number of Elastic IPs per account per Region by default, and how does this affect NAT Gateway planning? ; Default is 5 EIPs per account per Region (soft limit). Each NAT Gateway requires one EIP, so by default you can only create 5 NAT Gateways per Region without a limit increase. <br><br> *For multi-AZ deployments with one NAT GW per AZ (3 AZs = 3 EIPs), plus other EIP uses, the 5 EIP default can be a binding constraint.<br> *Request EIP limit increases via AWS Support before hitting the limit, especially for large multi-AZ, multi-VPC deployments.<br> *Alternatively, use a centralized egress VPC via TGW to reduce the total number of NAT Gateways (and EIPs) required across the organization.<br>

91. [ Direct Connect ] A retail company processes holiday traffic spikes with 10x normal bandwidth. They have a 1 Gbps Direct Connect. What should they plan for? ; DX bandwidth is fixed at provisioned speed — there is no burst capability. For spikes, add Site-to-Site VPN as supplemental capacity, or upgrade to higher-bandwidth connection (10G), or use Hosted Connections with on-demand bandwidth. <br><br> *Unlike AWS services that scale elastically, Direct Connect has a fixed bandwidth ceiling at the provisioned port speed — 1 Gbps cannot burst to 10 Gbps.<br> *VPN over the internet can supplement DX capacity during spikes, but with higher latency and variable throughput.<br> *For variable workloads, consider architecting so that burst traffic goes directly to AWS over the internet (with appropriate security) while steady-state traffic uses DX.<br>

92. [ Internet Gateway ] A company's security team requires that all outbound internet traffic from EC2 instances be logged and inspectable. What architecture achieves this? ; Route all outbound traffic through AWS Network Firewall or a Gateway Load Balancer (GWLB) appliance in a centralized Inspection VPC via Transit Gateway, before reaching NAT Gateway and IGW. Enable VPC Flow Logs and Network Firewall logging to CloudWatch/S3. <br><br> *AWS Network Firewall provides stateful deep packet inspection, domain-based filtering, and IDS/IPS signatures for outbound traffic inspection.<br> *GWLB with third-party firewall appliances (Palo Alto, Fortinet) provides enterprise-grade inspection while maintaining original IP addressing via GENEVE encapsulation.<br> *VPC Flow Logs capture metadata (source/destination IP, port, bytes, action) but not payload — for payload inspection, a dedicated firewall appliance is required.<br>

93. [ Subnets ] What is the difference between a subnet's "auto-assign public IPv4 address" setting and an Elastic IP? ; Auto-assign: automatically assigns a temporary dynamic public IP to instances at launch; IP changes every time the instance is stopped and started. Elastic IP: static persistent public IP that remains associated until explicitly released. <br><br> *Auto-assigned IPs are returned to AWS when the instance stops; any DNS records or external references to that IP will break after a stop/start cycle.<br> *Elastic IPs are free when associated with a running instance; they incur a small hourly charge when unassociated (allocated but not used) — to avoid charges, release EIPs not in use.<br> *For internet-facing web servers, DNS, or any service requiring a stable public IP, always use Elastic IPs instead of auto-assigned IPs.<br>

94. [ Direct Connect ] What is the recommended approach for testing Direct Connect failover to a backup VPN without causing production outage? ; Use BGP route manipulation in a maintenance window: increase MED or prepend AS path on DX advertisements to make VPN temporarily preferred. Monitor traffic shifting to VPN, verify connectivity, then restore DX as primary. <br><br> *This approach tests the entire failover path (BGP convergence + VPN throughput + application connectivity) without actually breaking the DX connection.<br> *Never test failover by physically disconnecting DX in production without a tested backup — validate the backup path first using BGP manipulation.<br> *Ideally, test failover in a non-production environment first, then apply the same test procedure to production in a scheduled maintenance window with stakeholder approval.<br>

95. [ Internet Gateway ] What is the relationship between VPC Flow Logs and Internet Gateway? ; VPC Flow Logs can capture traffic at the VPC, subnet, or ENI level, including traffic flowing through the IGW. Flow Logs record accepted and rejected traffic for all IP traffic to/from network interfaces. <br><br> *Flow Logs capture metadata (src IP, dst IP, ports, protocol, bytes, action, status) but not packet payload — they are a connection log, not a packet capture.<br> *For IGW traffic, Flow Logs on the VPC or public subnet capture both inbound internet traffic (allowed or denied by SG/NACL) and outbound internet traffic from public instances.<br> *Flow Log data is published to CloudWatch Logs, S3, or Kinesis Data Firehose; query with CloudWatch Insights or Athena for traffic analysis and security investigations.<br>

96. [ Subnets ] Why should database subnets have completely isolated route tables with only the local VPC route? ; Databases should never initiate or receive internet connections. Isolated subnets prevent accidental exposure even if security groups are misconfigured or route tables are modified by operators. <br><br> *A route table with only the local VPC route (10.0.0.0/16 → local) means even if someone adds an internet-routable security group rule, packets cannot leave the VPC — no route exists.<br> *This is defense-in-depth: route table isolation is at the network layer, below security groups (which operate at the instance layer).<br> *AWS calls these "isolated" subnets in the CDK VPC construct (SubnetType.PRIVATE_ISOLATED) to distinguish them from private subnets that have NAT GW access.<br>

97. [ Internet Gateway ] Can an Internet Gateway be shared between two VPCs? ; No. An Internet Gateway is attached to exactly one VPC at a time. Each VPC that needs internet connectivity must have its own IGW. <br><br> *IGW is a VPC-level resource — one-to-one relationship with a VPC. You can detach it and reattach to another VPC, but it can only serve one VPC at any given time.<br> *For internet access sharing across VPCs, use a centralized Egress VPC with Transit Gateway: all VPCs route internet traffic to the central Egress VPC which has the IGW and NAT GW.<br> *This centralized pattern provides a single exit point for security inspection and consistent source IPs, while sharing one set of NAT GW + IGW resources.<br>

98. [ Direct Connect ] A company has DX and wants on-premises systems to resolve AWS private DNS names (EC2 private hostnames, RDS endpoints). What is required? ; Deploy Route 53 Resolver (Inbound Endpoint) in the VPC. Configure on-premises DNS to forward AWS domain queries (.internal, .compute.internal, specific RDS hostnames) to the Resolver inbound endpoint IPs over DX. <br><br> *Route 53 Resolver Inbound Endpoints are ENIs in your VPC that accept DNS queries from on-premises; on-premises DNS servers forward specific domains to these IPs over the DX connection.<br> *Without this, on-premises systems cannot resolve AWS private DNS names because these names resolve to private IPs only visible from within the VPC.<br> *Route 53 Resolver Outbound Endpoints enable the reverse: VPC instances forwarding queries for on-premises domains to on-premises DNS servers via DX.<br>

99. [ Internet Gateway ] What is the purpose of "Private DNS" setting on a VPC Interface Endpoint? ; When Private DNS is enabled, the standard public DNS hostname for the service (e.g., sqs.us-east-1.amazonaws.com) resolves to the Interface Endpoint's private IP within the VPC, overriding the public IP resolution. <br><br> *Without Private DNS: the service FQDN resolves to public IPs both inside and outside the VPC — you must use the endpoint-specific DNS name to use the Interface Endpoint.<br> *With Private DNS: any code using the standard service SDK endpoint URL automatically uses the private Interface Endpoint without code changes — transparent to applications.<br> *Private DNS requires "Enable DNS Hostnames" and "Enable DNS Support" on the VPC; if these are disabled, Private DNS on endpoints will not work.<br>

100. [ Direct Connect ] What is the typical lead time for provisioning an AWS Direct Connect Dedicated connection? ; Typically 4-12 weeks for a Dedicated connection (requires physical cross-connect installation at DX colocation facility). Hosted connections from partners can be provisioned in days to weeks. <br><br> *Dedicated connections require the DX colocation facility to install a physical cross-connect between your/partner cage and the AWS router — this involves scheduling facility work, which takes weeks.<br> *Hosted connections from existing DX Partners are faster because the partner already has physical connectivity to AWS; they just provision a logical VIF on their existing infrastructure.<br> *Always plan DX provisioning well in advance of project deadlines — a 12-week lead time is common, and surprises (facility delays, LOA issues) can extend this further.<br>

101. [ Subnets ] What is the difference between IPv4 and IPv6 subnet sizing rules in AWS? ; IPv4: subnets can be /16 to /28 (you choose the size). IPv6: subnets must be exactly /64 (no choice, automatically assigned from the VPC's /56). <br><br> *The /64 fixed size for IPv6 subnets means every IPv6 subnet has 2^64 addresses — effectively unlimited, so subnet sizing is not a constraint for IPv6.<br> *VPC IPv6 CIDR is /56, automatically assigned from Amazon's IPv6 pool (or BYOIP); this gives 256 possible /64 subnets per VPC (/56 - /64 = 8 bits = 256 subnets).<br> *You cannot choose a custom IPv6 CIDR size for subnets; AWS enforces /64 to comply with IPv6 standards that assume /64 for a single network segment (required for SLAAC).<br>

102. [ Internet Gateway ] A company migrates from NAT Instance to NAT Gateway. What are the key differences they should know about? ; NAT Gateway: fully managed, auto-scales to 100 Gbps, no security groups, no SSH access, requires EIP, charges per hour + per GB. NAT Instance: EC2-based, manual management, supports security groups, can be used as bastion host, limited bandwidth, must disable source/dest check. <br><br> *NAT Gateway eliminates operational overhead: no patching, no instance type upgrades, no HA clustering — AWS manages all of this automatically.<br> *NAT Instance's main advantage is flexibility: you can customize iptables rules, enable port forwarding, and use it as a bastion host — capabilities NAT Gateway doesn't have.<br> *For production workloads, NAT Gateway is almost always the right choice; NAT Instance is a legacy option still used for specific use cases like port forwarding requirements.<br>

103.
